    "originalFile": "\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:experiments}\n\n\\subsection{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{sec:setup}\n\nWe study iterative training collapse using Pythia-410M~\\citep{biderman2023pythia} as the primary architecture.\nTraining data is drawn from WikiText-103~\\citep{merity2017pointer} following the replace paradigm of~\\citet{shumailov2024curse}: each generation fine-tunes on a mixture of $\\alpha$ synthetic and $(1{-}\\alpha)$ real data, generates 5{,}000 replacement samples, and fully replaces the synthetic pool (hyperparameters in Appendix~\\ref{app:training_details}).\nWe run $T{=}10$ generations across 13 contamination levels ($\\alpha \\in \\{0.00, \\ldots, 1.00\\}$) with multi-seed replication, yielding ${\\sim}1{,}500$ statistical tests after BH-FDR correction~\\citep{benjamini1995controlling}.\nWe track two \\emph{canary metrics} (token entropy and ECE) and three \\emph{downstream metrics}: distinct-1, distinct-2~\\citep{li2016diversity}, and perplexity (distinct-3: Appendix~\\ref{app:distinct3}).\nPermG uses 5{,}000 permutations on first-differenced series~\\citep{shojaie2022granger} with maximum lag~5.\n\n\n\\subsection{Sharp Detection Onset}\n\\label{sec:regime}\n\nDiversity collapse occurs at every contamination level: distinct-1 declines by $14.1\\%$ even at $\\alpha{=}0.0$ (fine-tuning drift) and by $40.3{\\pm}0.3\\%$ at $\\alpha{=}0.25$.\nDespite this ubiquitous degradation, Granger detectability is sharply regime-dependent.\nPermG finds 0/8 canary$\\to$downstream pairs FDR-significant from $\\alpha{=}0.00$ through $\\alpha{=}0.45$, then jumps to 6/8 (Clopper--Pearson 95\\% CI $[0.25, 0.92]$) at $\\alpha{=}0.50$, uniformly across all 3~fp32 seeds.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/metric_trajectory.pdf}\n\\caption{Metric trajectories at $\\alpha{=}1.0$ (Pythia-410M, 4~seeds). Token entropy drops at generation~1; distinct-1 declines from generations~2--3, with a consistent one-generation canary lead (shaded: inter-seed range).}\n\\label{fig:metric_trajectory}\n\\end{figure}\n\nThis transition is striking because cumulative synthetic fraction barely changes across the boundary ($C{=}99.75\\%$ at $\\alpha{=}0.45$ vs.\\ $99.90\\%$ at $\\alpha{=}0.50$), yet detection jumps from null to reliable.\nSub-threshold $p$-values at $\\alpha{=}0.45$ ($p_{\\mathrm{fdr}} = 0.059$--$0.091$) confirm proximity to the boundary.\nSigmoid regression yields $\\alpha_c = 0.483 \\pm 0.006$ ($R^2 = 0.969$; Figure~\\ref{fig:dose_response}); cross-seed consistency (3/3 seeds: $0/8{\\to}6/8$) and specification-invariance across 1{,}160 configurations (Appendix~\\ref{app:spec_curve}) argue for a genuine transition (Fisher exact: $p = 3.45 \\times 10^{-11}$).\n\n\\paragraph{Canary scoping.}\nFirst-differencing scopes the canary claim precisely.\nCanary$\\to$diversity pairs retain unanimous FDR significance after first-differencing ($24/24 \\to 24/24$ across 4~seeds at $\\alpha{=}1.0$), while canary$\\to$perplexity collapses entirely ($F \\approx 0$, $p = 0.99$)~\\citep{granger1974spurious}.\nAt $\\alpha{=}1.0$, all 24 canary$\\to$diversity pairs reach FDR significance in the forward direction with 0/24 reverse; entropy onset at generation~1 precedes distinct-$n$ at generation~2 in 16/18 WikiText conditions (Appendix~\\ref{app:moment_cascade}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[htb]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_cascade_onset.pdf}\n\\caption{Cascade onset timeline (Pythia-410M, WikiText, fp32). Entropy and ECE reach onset at generation~1; distinct-$n$ follows at generation~2. The ordering $t_H \\leq t_D$ holds across all conditions.}\n\\label{fig:cascade_onset}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{Blind zone.}\nAt $\\alpha{=}0.10$, distinct-1 has already dropped by $39.6\\%$, yet token entropy is indistinguishable from the null.\nBelow $\\alpha_c$ at $T{=}10$, diversity collapse proceeds without any detectable Granger-precedence signal.\n\n\\FloatBarrier\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_spec_curve.pdf}\n\\caption{Specification curve (1{,}160 configurations). The blind$\\to$onset jump at $\\alpha_c{\\approx}0.50$ is robust across methods, pairs, and seeds.}\n\\label{fig:spec_curve}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\n\\subsection{Dose-Response and Detection Regimes}\n\\label{sec:dose_response}\n\nThe PermG dose-response profile (Figure~\\ref{fig:dose_response}; Appendix~\\ref{app:dose_response}) reveals three confirmed regimes---\\emph{blind} ($\\alpha \\leq 0.45$, 0/8), \\emph{onset} ($\\alpha \\approx 0.50$, 6/8), and \\emph{detection} ($\\alpha{\\geq}0.75$, 6/8)---plus a non-monotonic anomaly at $\\alpha \\approx 0.60$ (0/8).\nAt this level, all direction-checked methods fail (PermG 0/8, threshold onset 0/8, Toda--Yamamoto 1/8), while bidirectional DiffXCorr maintains detection (6/8), suggesting compressed temporal lag disrupts directional separation.\nThe anomaly dissolves by $T{=}25$ ($5/8$), consistent with $\\sqrt{T}$ power scaling (Appendix~\\ref{app:t20_comparison}).\n\n\n\\subsection{Detection Boundary Left-Shift}\n\\label{sec:t20}\n\nLonger observation windows push the detection boundary leftward: $\\alpha_c(5){\\geq}1.0$, $\\alpha_c(10){=}0.487$, consistent with power-law scaling (\\S\\ref{sec:theory}).\nThe $\\alpha{=}0.45$ column validates this: undetectable at $T{\\leq}10$ (0/8), it crosses into reliable detection at $T{=}15$ (6/8, dual-seed) and remains there at $T{=}20$ and $T{=}25$.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_dose_response.pdf}\n\\caption{Dose-response: PermG detection rate with sigmoid fit ($\\alpha_c{=}0.483$; shaded 95\\% CI). Regime bands mark blind, onset, sync-dip, and detection zones.}\n\\label{fig:dose_response}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Architecture Validation}\n\\label{sec:generalization}\n\nWe evaluate PermG at $\\alpha{=}1.0$ across 15 model-seed combinations spanning five families (345M--6.9B) and two domains (Table~\\ref{tab:generalization}; Appendix~\\ref{app:cross_arch_table}).\nForward detection reaches ${\\geq}6/8$ in 11/15 conditions (Figure~\\ref{fig:cross_arch_heatmap}).\nThe four non-confirming conditions each isolate a distinct failure mode: precision sensitivity (GPT-2 bf16: 0/8 vs.\\ fp32: 6/8), bidirectional confounding (GPT-2 s43), seed dependence (Pythia-1.4B: s42 2/8, s43 6/8), and entropy decoupling (SmolLM3-3B: 3/8).\nStandalone PermG claims require architecture-specific null calibration (GPT-2 null FPR: 50\\%; Appendix~\\ref{app:fpr_independence}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[htb]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_cross_arch_heatmap.pdf}\n\\caption{Cross-architecture validation ($\\alpha{=}1.0$): PermG forward detection (${\\geq}6/8$) in 11/15 model-seed conditions spanning five families (345M--6.9B); faded bars: non-confirming conditions.}\n\\label{fig:cross_arch_heatmap}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\n\\subsection{Canary-Based Intervention (Proof of Concept)}\n\\label{sec:intervention}\n\nAs a proof-of-concept under favorable conditions ($\\alpha{=}1.0$), we implement predictive cessation: training switches to pure real data when token entropy drops ${>}15\\%$ from generation~0 (robust band $[10\\%, 22\\%]$; Appendix~\\ref{app:intervention_sensitivity}).\nThe predictive trigger fires at generation~1 (one generation before a reactive distinct-1 baseline), reducing peak perplexity by 64\\% ($56.99$ vs.\\ $157.09$; Figure~\\ref{fig:intervention}) in both Pythia-410M and 1.4B.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_intervention.pdf}\n\\caption{Canary-triggered intervention (Pythia-410M, $\\alpha{=}1.0$). Predictive cessation on entropy (generation~1) reduces peak perplexity by 64\\% versus reactive triggering on distinct-1 (generation~2).}\n\\label{fig:intervention}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Detection outcomes across contamination levels (Pythia-410M, seed~42, $T{=}10$, fp32). The blind$\\to$onset jump at $\\alpha_c{\\approx}0.50$ is robust across methods, pairs, and seeds.}\n\\label{fig:spec_curve}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\n\\subsection{Dose-Response and Detection Regimes}\n\\label{sec:dose_response}\n\nThe PermG dose-response profile (Figure~\\ref{fig:dose_response}; Appendix~\\ref{app:dose_response}) reveals three confirmed regimes---\\emph{blind} ($\\alpha \\leq 0.45$, 0/8), \\emph{onset} ($\\alpha \\approx 0.50$, 6/8), and \\emph{detection} ($\\alpha{\\geq}0.75$, 6/8)---plus a non-monotonic anomaly at $\\alpha \\approx 0.60$ (0/8).\nAt this level, all direction-checked methods fail (PermG 0/8, threshold onset 0/8, Toda--Yamamoto 1/8), while bidirectional DiffXCorr maintains detection (6/8), suggesting compressed temporal lag disrupts directional separation.\nThe anomaly dissolves by $T{=}25$ ($5/8$), consistent with $\\sqrt{T}$ power scaling (Appendix~\\ref{app:t20_comparison}).\n\n\n\\subsection{Detection Boundary Left-Shift}\n\\label{sec:t20}\n\nLonger observation windows push the detection boundary leftward: $\\alpha_c(5){\\geq}1.0$, $\\alpha_c(10){=}0.487$, consistent with power-law scaling (\\S\\ref{sec:theory}).\nThe $\\alpha{=}0.45$ column validates this: undetectable at $T{\\leq}10$ (0/8), it crosses into reliable detection at $T{=}15$ (6/8, dual-seed) and remains there at $T{=}20$ and $T{=}25$.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_dose_response.pdf}\n\\caption{Dose-response: PermG detection rate with sigmoid fit ($\\alpha_c{=}0.483$; shaded 95\\% CI). Regime bands mark blind, onset, sync-dip, and detection zones.}\n\\label{fig:dose_response}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Architecture Validation}\n\\label{sec:generalization}\n\nWe evaluate PermG at $\\alpha{=}1.0$ across 15 model-seed combinations spanning five families (345M--6.9B) and two domains (Table~\\ref{tab:generalization}; Appendix~\\ref{app:cross_arch_table}).\nForward detection reaches ${\\geq}6/8$ in 11/15 conditions (Figure~\\ref{fig:cross_arch_heatmap}).\nThe four non-confirming conditions each isolate a distinct failure mode: precision sensitivity (GPT-2 bf16: 0/8 vs.\\ fp32: 6/8), bidirectional confounding (GPT-2 s43), seed dependence (Pythia-1.4B: s42 2/8, s43 6/8), and entropy decoupling (SmolLM3-3B: 3/8).\nStandalone PermG claims require architecture-specific null calibration (GPT-2 null FPR: 50\\%; Appendix~\\ref{app:fpr_independence}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[htb]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_cross_arch_heatmap.pdf}\n\\caption{Cross-architecture validation ($\\alpha{=}1.0$): PermG forward detection (${\\geq}6/8$) in 11/15 model-seed conditions spanning five families (345M--6.9B); faded bars: non-confirming conditions.}\n\\label{fig:cross_arch_heatmap}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\n\\subsection{Canary-Based Intervention (Proof of Concept)}\n\\label{sec:intervention}\n\nAs a proof-of-concept under favorable conditions ($\\alpha{=}1.0$), we implement predictive cessation: training switches to pure real data when token entropy drops ${>}15\\%$ from generation~0 (robust band $[10\\%, 22\\%]$; Appendix~\\ref{app:intervention_sensitivity}).\nThe predictive trigger fires at generation~1 (one generation before a reactive distinct-1 baseline), reducing peak perplexity by 64\\% ($56.99$ vs.\\ $157.09$; Figure~\\ref{fig:intervention}) in both Pythia-410M and 1.4B.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_intervention.pdf}\n\\caption{Canary-triggered intervention (Pythia-410M, $\\alpha{=}1.0$). Predictive cessation on entropy (generation~1) reduces peak perplexity by 64\\% versus reactive triggering on distinct-1 (generation~2).}\n\\label{fig:intervention}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Detection outcomes across contamination levels (Pythia-410M, seed~42, $T{=}10$, fp32). PermG: forward FDR-significant pairs; $K{\\geq}3$: consensus pairs (of 8 canary$\\to$downstream pairs).}\n\\label{tab:summary_detection}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}\n\\toprule\n$\\alpha$ & PermG & $K{\\geq}3$ & Regime \\\\\n\\midrule\n0.00 & 0/8 & 0/8 & null \\\\\n0.25 & 0/8 & 0/8 & blind \\\\\n0.45 & 0/8 & 0/8 & blind \\\\\n0.50 & 6/8 & 6/8 & onset \\\\\n0.60 & 0/8 & 0/8 & anomaly \\\\\n0.75 & 6/8 & 6/8 & detection \\\\\n1.00 & 6/8 & 6/8 & detection \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Consensus threshold operating characteristics ($K \\in \\{2,3,4,5\\}$). FPR: null-control rate (9 seeds, 54 pairs); TPR: detection rate at selected $\\alpha$ levels (Pythia-410M, $T{=}10$, fp32). $K{=}3$ balances FPR control (11.1\\%) against detection power (75\\% at $\\alpha{=}1.0$); $K{=}4$ eliminates false positives but reduces TPR to ${\\leq}25\\%$.}\n\\label{tab:mmct_k_operating}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\n& $K{\\geq}2$ & $K{\\geq}3$ & $K{\\geq}4$ & $K{=}5$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nFPR ($\\alpha{=}0.0$) & 38.9\\% & 11.1\\% & 0\\% & 0\\% \\\\\nTPR ($\\alpha{=}0.50$) & 85.4\\% & 66.7\\% & 25.0\\% & 4.2\\% \\\\\nTPR ($\\alpha{=}0.75$) & 72.9\\% & 37.5\\% & 0\\% & 0\\% \\\\\nTPR ($\\alpha{=}1.0$) & 75.0\\% & 75.0\\% & 25.0\\% & 0\\% \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n",

75	\label{fig:dose_response}

78	
79	\subsection{Cross-Architecture Validation}
80	\label{sec:generalization}
81	
82	We evaluate PermG at $\alpha{=}1.0$ across 15 model-seed combinations spanning five families (345M--6.9B) and two domains (Table~\ref{tab:generalization}; Appendix~\ref{app:cross_arch_table}).
83	Forward detection reaches ${\geq}6/8$ in 11/15 conditions (Figure~\ref{fig:cross_arch_heatmap}).
84	The four non-confirming conditions each isolate a distinct failure mode: precision sensitivity (GPT-2 bf16: 0/8 vs.\ fp32: 6/8), bidirectional confounding (GPT-2 s43), seed dependence (Pythia-1.4B: s42 2/8, s43 6/8), and entropy decoupling (SmolLM3-3B: 3/8).
85	Standalone PermG claims require architecture-specific null calibration (GPT-2 null FPR: 50\%; Appendix~\ref{app:fpr_independence}).
86	
87	\begin{figure}[htb]
88	\centering
89	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig_cross_arch_heatmap.pdf}
90	\caption{Cross-architecture validation ($\alpha{=}1.0$): PermG forward detection (${\geq}6/8$) in 11/15 model-seed conditions spanning five families (345M--6.9B); faded bars: non-confirming conditions.}
91	\label{fig:cross_arch_heatmap}
92	\end{figure}
93	
94	
95	\subsection{Canary-Based Intervention (Proof of Concept)}
96	\label{sec:intervention}
97	
98	As a proof-of-concept under favorable conditions ($\alpha{=}1.0$), we implement predictive cessation: training switches to pure real data when token entropy drops ${>}15\%$ from generation~0 (robust band $[10\%, 22\%]$; Appendix~\ref{app:intervention_sensitivity}).
99	The predictive trigger fires at generation~1 (one generation before a reactive distinct-1 baseline), reducing peak perplexity by 64\% ($56.99$ vs.\ $157.09$; Figure~\ref{fig:intervention}) in both Pythia-410M and 1.4B.
100	
101	\begin{figure}[t]
102	\centering
103	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig_intervention.pdf}
104	\caption{Canary-triggered intervention (Pythia-410M, $\alpha{=}1.0$). Predictive cessation on entropy (generation~1) reduces peak perplexity by 64\% versus reactive triggering on distinct-1 (generation~2).}
105	\label{fig:intervention}
106	\end{figure}
107	
108	\begin{table}[t]
109	\centering
110	\caption{Detection outcomes across contamination levels (Pythia-410M, seed~42, $T{=}10$, fp32). PermG: forward FDR-significant pairs; $K{\geq}3$: consensus pairs (of 8 canary$\to$downstream pairs).}
111	\label{tab:summary_detection}

116	0.00 & 0/8 & 0/8 & null \\
117	0.25 & 0/8 & 0/8 & blind \\
118	0.45 & 0/8 & 0/8 & blind \\
119	0.50 & 6/8 & 6/8 & onset \\
120	0.60 & 0/8 & 0/8 & anomaly \\
121	0.75 & 6/8 & 6/8 & detection \\
122	1.00 & 6/8 & 6/8 & detection \\
123	\bottomrule

125	\end{table}

127	\begin{table}[t]
128	\centering
129	\caption{Consensus threshold operating characteristics ($K \in \{2,3,4,5\}$). FPR: null-control rate (9 seeds, 54 pairs); TPR: detection rate at selected $\alpha$ levels (Pythia-410M, $T{=}10$, fp32). $K{=}3$ balances FPR control (11.1\%) against detection power (75\% at $\alpha{=}1.0$); $K{=}4$ eliminates false positives but reduces TPR to ${\leq}25\%$.}

      "content": "\\label{fig:dose_response}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Architecture Validation}\n\\label{sec:generalization}\n\nWe evaluate PermG at $\\alpha{=}1.0$ across 15 model-seed combinations spanning five families (345M--6.9B) and two domains (Table~\\ref{tab:generalization}; Appendix~\\ref{app:cross_arch_table}).\nForward detection reaches ${\\geq}6/8$ in 11/15 conditions (Figure~\\ref{fig:cross_arch_heatmap}).\nThe four non-confirming conditions each isolate a distinct failure mode: precision sensitivity (GPT-2 bf16: 0/8 vs.\\ fp32: 6/8), bidirectional confounding (GPT-2 s43), seed dependence (Pythia-1.4B: s42 2/8, s43 6/8), and entropy decoupling (SmolLM3-3B: 3/8).\nStandalone PermG claims require architecture-specific null calibration (GPT-2 null FPR: 50\\%; Appendix~\\ref{app:fpr_independence}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[htb]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_cross_arch_heatmap.pdf}\n\\caption{Cross-architecture validation ($\\alpha{=}1.0$): PermG forward detection (${\\geq}6/8$) in 11/15 model-seed conditions spanning five families (345M--6.9B); faded bars: non-confirming conditions.}\n\\label{fig:cross_arch_heatmap}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\n\\subsection{Canary-Based Intervention (Proof of Concept)}\n\\label{sec:intervention}\n\nAs a proof-of-concept under favorable conditions ($\\alpha{=}1.0$), we implement predictive cessation: training switches to pure real data when token entropy drops ${>}15\\%$ from generation~0 (robust band $[10\\%, 22\\%]$; Appendix~\\ref{app:intervention_sensitivity}).\nThe predictive trigger fires at generation~1 (one generation before a reactive distinct-1 baseline), reducing peak perplexity by 64\\% ($56.99$ vs.\\ $157.09$; Figure~\\ref{fig:intervention}) in both Pythia-410M and 1.4B.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_intervention.pdf}\n\\caption{Canary-triggered intervention (Pythia-410M, $\\alpha{=}1.0$). Predictive cessation on entropy (generation~1) reduces peak perplexity by 64\\% versus reactive triggering on distinct-1 (generation~2).}\n\\label{fig:intervention}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Detection outcomes across contamination levels (Pythia-410M, seed~42, $T{=}10$, fp32). PermG: forward FDR-significant pairs; $K{\\geq}3$: consensus pairs (of 8 canary$\\to$downstream pairs).}\n\\label{tab:summary_detection}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}\n\\toprule\n$\\alpha$ & PermG & $K{\\geq}3$ & Regime \\\\\n\\midrule\n0.00 & 0/8 & 0/8 & null \\\\\n0.25 & 0/8 & 0/8 & blind \\\\\n0.45 & 0/8 & 0/8 & blind \\\\\n0.50 & 6/8 & 6/8 & onset \\\\\n0.60 & 0/8 & 0/8 & anomaly \\\\\n0.75 & 6/8 & 6/8 & detection \\\\\n1.00 & 6/8 & 6/8 & detection \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Consensus threshold operating characteristics ($K \\in \\{2,3,4,5\\}$). FPR: null-control rate (9 seeds, 54 pairs); TPR: detection rate at selected $\\alpha$ levels (Pythia-410M, $T{=}10$, fp32). $K{=}3$ balances FPR control (11.1\\%) against detection power (75\\% at $\\alpha{=}1.0$); $K{=}4$ eliminates false positives but reduces TPR to ${\\leq}25\\%$.}\n\\label{tab:mmct_k_operating}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\n& $K{\\geq}2$ & $K{\\geq}3$ & $K{\\geq}4$ & $K{=}5$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nFPR ($\\alpha{=}0.0$) & 38.9\\% & 11.1\\% & 0\\% & 0\\% \\\\\nTPR ($\\alpha{=}0.50$) & 85.4\\% & 66.7\\% & 25.0\\% & 4.2\\% \\\\\nTPR ($\\alpha{=}0.75$) & 72.9\\% & 37.5\\% & 0\\% & 0\\% \\\\\nTPR ($\\alpha{=}1.0$) & 75.0\\% & 75.0\\% & 25.0\\% & 0\\% \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:experiments}\n\n\\subsection{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{sec:setup}\n\nWe study iterative training collapse using Pythia-410M~\\citep{biderman2023pythia} as the primary architecture.\nTraining data is drawn from WikiText-103~\\citep{merity2017pointer} following the replace paradigm of~\\citet{shumailov2024curse}: each generation fine-tunes on a mixture of $\\alpha$ synthetic and $(1{-}\\alpha)$ real data, generates 5{,}000 replacement samples, and fully replaces the synthetic pool (hyperparameters in Appendix~\\ref{app:training_details}).\nWe run $T{=}10$ generations across 13 contamination levels ($\\alpha \\in \\{0.00, \\ldots, 1.00\\}$) with multi-seed replication, yielding ${\\sim}1{,}500$ statistical tests after BH-FDR correction~\\citep{benjamini1995controlling}.\nWe track two \\emph{canary metrics} (token entropy and ECE) and three \\emph{downstream metrics}: distinct-1, distinct-2~\\citep{li2016diversity}, and perplexity (distinct-3: Appendix~\\ref{app:distinct3}).\nPermG uses 5{,}000 permutations on first-differenced series~\\citep{shojaie2022granger} with maximum lag~5.\n\n\n\\subsection{Sharp Detection Onset}\n\\label{sec:regime}\n\nDiversity collapse occurs at every contamination level: distinct-1 declines by $14.1\\%$ even at $\\alpha{=}0.0$ (fine-tuning drift) and by $40.3{\\pm}0.3\\%$ at $\\alpha{=}0.25$.\nDespite this ubiquitous degradation, Granger detectability is sharply regime-dependent.\nPermG finds 0/8 canary$\\to$downstream pairs FDR-significant from $\\alpha{=}0.00$ through $\\alpha{=}0.45$, then jumps to 6/8 (Clopper--Pearson 95\\% CI $[0.25, 0.92]$) at $\\alpha{=}0.50$, uniformly across all 3~fp32 seeds.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/metric_trajectory.pdf}\n\\caption{Metric trajectories at $\\alpha{=}1.0$ (Pythia-410M, 4~seeds). Token entropy drops at generation~1; distinct-1 declines from generations~2--3, with a consistent one-generation canary lead (shaded: inter-seed range).}\n\\label{fig:metric_trajectory}\n\\end{figure}\n\nThis transition is striking because cumulative synthetic fraction barely changes across the boundary ($C{=}99.75\\%$ at $\\alpha{=}0.45$ vs.\\ $99.90\\%$ at $\\alpha{=}0.50$), yet detection jumps from null to reliable.\nSub-threshold $p$-values at $\\alpha{=}0.45$ ($p_{\\mathrm{fdr}} = 0.059$--$0.091$) confirm proximity to the boundary.\nSigmoid regression yields $\\alpha_c = 0.483 \\pm 0.006$ ($R^2 = 0.969$; Figure~\\ref{fig:dose_response}); cross-seed consistency (3/3 seeds: $0/8{\\to}6/8$) and specification-invariance across 1{,}160 configurations (Appendix~\\ref{app:spec_curve}) argue for a genuine transition (Fisher exact: $p = 3.45 \\times 10^{-11}$).\n\n\\paragraph{Canary scoping.}\nFirst-differencing scopes the canary claim precisely.\nCanary$\\to$diversity pairs retain unanimous FDR significance after first-differencing ($24/24 \\to 24/24$ across 4~seeds at $\\alpha{=}1.0$), while canary$\\to$perplexity collapses entirely ($F \\approx 0$, $p = 0.99$)~\\citep{granger1974spurious}.\nAt $\\alpha{=}1.0$, all 24 canary$\\to$diversity pairs reach FDR significance in the forward direction with 0/24 reverse; entropy onset at generation~1 precedes distinct-$n$ at generation~2 in 16/18 WikiText conditions (Appendix~\\ref{app:moment_cascade}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[htb]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_cascade_onset.pdf}\n\\caption{Cascade onset timeline (Pythia-410M, WikiText, fp32). Entropy and ECE reach onset at generation~1; distinct-$n$ follows at generation~2. The ordering $t_H \\leq t_D$ holds across all conditions.}\n\\label{fig:cascade_onset}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{Blind zone.}\nAt $\\alpha{=}0.10$, distinct-1 has already dropped by $39.6\\%$, yet token entropy is indistinguishable from the null.\nBelow $\\alpha_c$ at $T{=}10$, diversity collapse proceeds without any detectable Granger-precedence signal.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_spec_curve.pdf}\n\\caption{Specification curve (1{,}160 configurations). The blind$\\to$onset jump at $\\alpha_c{\\approx}0.50$ is robust across methods, pairs, and seeds.}\n\\label{fig:spec_curve}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\n\\subsection{Dose-Response and Detection Regimes}\n\\label{sec:dose_response}\n\nThe PermG dose-response profile (Figure~\\ref{fig:dose_response}; Appendix~\\ref{app:dose_response}) reveals three confirmed regimes---\\emph{blind} ($\\alpha \\leq 0.45$, 0/8), \\emph{onset} ($\\alpha \\approx 0.50$, 6/8), and \\emph{detection} ($\\alpha{\\geq}0.75$, 6/8)---plus a non-monotonic anomaly at $\\alpha \\approx 0.60$ (0/8).\nAt this level, all direction-checked methods fail (PermG 0/8, threshold onset 0/8, Toda--Yamamoto 1/8), while bidirectional DiffXCorr maintains detection (6/8), suggesting compressed temporal lag disrupts directional separation.\nThe anomaly dissolves by $T{=}25$ ($5/8$), consistent with $\\sqrt{T}$ power scaling (Appendix~\\ref{app:t20_comparison}).\n\n\\FloatBarrier\n\n\\subsection{Detection Boundary Left-Shift}\n\\label{sec:t20}\n\nLonger observation windows push the detection boundary leftward: $\\alpha_c(5){\\geq}1.0$, $\\alpha_c(10){=}0.487$, consistent with power-law scaling (\\S\\ref{sec:theory}).\nThe $\\alpha{=}0.45$ column validates this: undetectable at $T{\\leq}10$ (0/8), it crosses into reliable detection at $T{=}15$ (6/8, dual-seed) and remains there at $T{=}20$ and $T{=}25$.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_dose_response.pdf}\n\\caption{Dose-response: PermG detection rate with sigmoid fit ($\\alpha_c{=}0.483$; shaded 95\\% CI). Regime bands mark blind, onset, sync-dip, and detection zones.}\n\\label{fig:dose_response}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Architecture Validation}\n\\label{sec:generalization}\n\nWe evaluate PermG at $\\alpha{=}1.0$ across 15 model-seed combinations spanning five families (345M--6.9B) and two domains (Table~\\ref{tab:generalization}; faded bars: non-confirming conditions.}\n\\label{fig:cross_arch_heatmap}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\n\\subsection{Canary-Based Intervention (Proof of Concept)}\n\\label{sec:intervention}\n\nAs a proof-of-concept under favorable conditions ($\\alpha{=}1.0$), we implement predictive cessation: training switches to pure real data when token entropy drops ${>}15\\%$ from generation~0 (robust band $[10\\%, 22\\%]$; Appendix~\\ref{app:intervention_sensitivity}).\nThe predictive trigger fires at generation~1 (one generation before a reactive distinct-1 baseline), reducing peak perplexity by 64\\% ($56.99$ vs.\\ $157.09$; Figure~\\ref{fig:intervention}) in both Pythia-410M and 1.4B.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_intervention.pdf}\n\\caption{Canary-triggered intervention (Pythia-410M, $\\alpha{=}1.0$).     "originalFile": "\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:experiments}\n\n\\subsection{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{sec:setup}\n\nWe study iterative training collapse using Pythia-410M~\\citep{biderman2023pythia} as the primary architecture.\nTraining data is drawn from WikiText-103~\\citep{merity2017pointer} following the replace paradigm of~\\citet{shumailov2024curse}: each generation fine-tunes on a mixture of $\\alpha$ synthetic and $(1{-}\\alpha)$ real data, generates 5{,}000 replacement samples, and fully replaces the synthetic pool (hyperparameters in Appendix~\\ref{app:training_details}).\nWe run $T{=}10$ generations across 13 contamination levels ($\\alpha \\in \\{0.00, \\ldots, 1.00\\}$) with multi-seed replication, yielding ${\\sim}1{,}500$ statistical tests after BH-FDR correction~\\citep{benjamini1995controlling}.\nWe track two \\emph{canary metrics} (token entropy and ECE) and three \\emph{downstream metrics}: distinct-1, distinct-2~\\citep{li2016diversity}, and perplexity (distinct-3: Appendix~\\ref{app:distinct3}).\nPermG uses 5{,}000 permutations on first-differenced series~\\citep{shojaie2022granger} with maximum lag~5.\n\n\n\\subsection{Sharp Detection Onset}\n\\label{sec:regime}\n\nDiversity collapse occurs at every contamination level: distinct-1 declines by $14.1\\%$ even at $\\alpha{=}0.0$ (fine-tuning drift) and by $40.3{\\pm}0.3\\%$ at $\\alpha{=}0.25$.\nDespite this ubiquitous degradation, Granger detectability is sharply regime-dependent.\nPermG finds 0/8 canary$\\to$downstream pairs FDR-significant from $\\alpha{=}0.00$ through $\\alpha{=}0.45$, then jumps to 6/8 (Clopper--Pearson 95\\% CI $[0.25, 0.92]$) at $\\alpha{=}0.50$, uniformly across all 3~fp32 seeds.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/metric_trajectory.pdf}\n\\caption{Metric trajectories at $\\alpha{=}1.0$ (Pythia-410M, 4~seeds). Token entropy drops at generation~1; distinct-1 declines from generations~2--3, with a consistent one-generation canary lead (shaded: inter-seed range).}\n\\label{fig:metric_trajectory}\n\\end{figure}\n\nThis transition is striking because cumulative synthetic fraction barely changes across the boundary ($C{=}99.75\\%$ at $\\alpha{=}0.45$ vs.\\ $99.90\\%$ at $\\alpha{=}0.50$), yet detection jumps from null to reliable.\nSub-threshold $p$-values at $\\alpha{=}0.45$ ($p_{\\mathrm{fdr}} = 0.059$--$0.091$) confirm proximity to the boundary.\nSigmoid regression yields $\\alpha_c = 0.483 \\pm 0.006$ ($R^2 = 0.969$; Figure~\\ref{fig:dose_response}); cross-seed consistency (3/3 seeds: $0/8{\\to}6/8$) and specification-invariance across 1{,}160 configurations (Appendix~\\ref{app:spec_curve}) argue for a genuine transition (Fisher exact: $p = 3.45 \\times 10^{-11}$).\n\n\\paragraph{Canary scoping.}\nFirst-differencing scopes the canary claim precisely.\nCanary$\\to$diversity pairs retain unanimous FDR significance after first-differencing ($24/24 \\to 24/24$ across 4~seeds at $\\alpha{=}1.0$), while canary$\\to$perplexity collapses entirely ($F \\approx 0$, $p = 0.99$)~\\citep{granger1974spurious}.\nAt $\\alpha{=}1.0$, all 24 canary$\\to$diversity pairs reach FDR significance in the forward direction with 0/24 reverse; entropy onset at generation~1 precedes distinct-$n$ at generation~2 in 16/18 WikiText conditions (Appendix~\\ref{app:moment_cascade}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[htb]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_cascade_onset.pdf}\n\\caption{Cascade onset timeline (Pythia-410M, WikiText, fp32). Entropy and ECE reach onset at generation~1; distinct-$n$ follows at generation~2. The ordering $t_H \\leq t_D$ holds across all conditions.}\n\\label{fig:cascade_onset}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{Blind zone.}\nAt $\\alpha{=}0.10$, distinct-1 has already dropped by $39.6\\%$, yet token entropy is indistinguishable from the null.\nBelow $\\alpha_c$ at $T{=}10$, diversity collapse proceeds without any detectable Granger-precedence signal.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_spec_curve.pdf}\n\\caption{Specification curve (1{,}160 configurations). The blind$\\to$onset jump at $\\alpha_c{\\approx}0.50$ is robust across methods, pairs, and seeds.}\n\\label{fig:spec_curve}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\n\\subsection{Dose-Response and Detection Regimes}\n\\label{sec:dose_response}\n\nThe PermG dose-response profile (Figure~\\ref{fig:dose_response}; Appendix~\\ref{app:dose_response}) reveals three confirmed regimes---\\emph{blind} ($\\alpha \\leq 0.45$, 0/8), \\emph{onset} ($\\alpha \\approx 0.50$, 6/8), and \\emph{detection} ($\\alpha{\\geq}0.75$, 6/8)---plus a non-monotonic anomaly at $\\alpha \\approx 0.60$ (0/8).\nAt this level, all direction-checked methods fail (PermG 0/8, threshold onset 0/8, Toda--Yamamoto 1/8), while bidirectional DiffXCorr maintains detection (6/8), suggesting compressed temporal lag disrupts directional separation.\nThe anomaly dissolves by $T{=}25$ ($5/8$), consistent with $\\sqrt{T}$ power scaling (Appendix~\\ref{app:t20_comparison}).\n\n\\FloatBarrier\n\n\\subsection{Detection Boundary Left-Shift}\n\\label{sec:t20}\n\nLonger observation windows push the detection boundary leftward: $\\alpha_c(5){\\geq}1.0$, $\\alpha_c(10){=}0.487$, consistent with power-law scaling (\\S\\ref{sec:theory}).\nThe $\\alpha{=}0.45$ column validates this: undetectable at $T{\\leq}10$ (0/8), it crosses into reliable detection at $T{=}15$ (6/8, dual-seed) and remains there at $T{=}20$ and $T{=}25$.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_dose_response.pdf}\n\\caption{Dose-response: PermG detection rate with sigmoid fit ($\\alpha_c{=}0.483$; shaded 95\\% CI). Regime bands mark blind, onset, sync-dip, and detection zones.}\n\\label{fig:dose_response}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Architecture Validation}\n\\label{sec:generalization}\n\nWe evaluate PermG at $\\alpha{=}1.0$ across 15 model-seed combinations spanning five families (345M--6.9B) and two domains (Table~\\ref{tab:generalization}; Appendix~\\ref{app:cross_arch_table}).\nForward detection reaches ${\\geq}6/8$ in 11/15 conditions (Figure~\\ref{fig:cross_arch_heatmap}).\nThe four non-confirming conditions each isolate a distinct failure mode: precision sensitivity (GPT-2 bf16: 0/8 vs.\\ fp32: 6/8), bidirectional confounding (GPT-2 s43), seed dependence (Pythia-1.4B: s42 2/8, s43 6/8), and entropy decoupling (SmolLM3-3B: 3/8).\nStandalone PermG claims require architecture-specific null calibration (GPT-2 null FPR: 50\\%; Appendix~\\ref{app:fpr_independence}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[htb]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_cross_arch_heatmap.pdf}\n\\caption{Cross-architecture validation ($\\alpha{=}1.0$): PermG forward detection (${\\geq}6/8$) in 11/15 model-seed conditions spanning five families (345M--6.9B); faded bars: non-confirming conditions.}\n\\label{fig:cross_arch_heatmap}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\FloatBarrier\n\n\\subsection{Canary-Based Intervention (Proof of Concept)}\n\\label{sec:intervention}\n\nAs a proof-of-concept under favorable conditions ($\\alpha{=}1.0$), we implement predictive cessation: training switches to pure real data when token entropy drops ${>}15\\%$ from generation~0 (robust band $[10\\%, 22\\%]$; Appendix~\\ref{app:intervention_sensitivity}).\nThe predictive trigger fires at generation~1 (one generation before a reactive distinct-1 baseline), reducing peak perplexity by 64\\% ($56.99$ vs.\\ $157.09$; Figure~\\ref{fig:intervention}) in both Pythia-410M and 1.4B.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_intervention.pdf}\n\\caption{Canary-triggered intervention (Pythia-410M, $\\alpha{=}1.0$). Predictive cessation on entropy (generation~1) reduces peak perplexity by 64\\% versus reactive triggering on distinct-1 (generation~2).}\n\\label{fig:intervention}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Detection outcomes across contamination levels (Pythia-410M, seed~42, $T{=}10$, fp32). PermG: forward FDR-significant pairs; $K{\\geq}3$: consensus pairs (of 8 canary$\\to$downstream pairs).}\n\\label{tab:summary_detection}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}\n\\toprule\n$\\alpha$ & PermG & $K{\\geq}3$ & Regime \\\\\n\\midrule\n0.00 & 0/8 & 0/8 & null \\\\\n0.25 & 0/8 & 0/8 & blind \\\\\n0.45 & 0/8 & 0/8 & blind \\\\\n0.50 & 6/8 & 6/8 & onset \\\\\n0.60 & 0/8 & 0/8 & anomaly \\\\\n0.75 & 6/8 & 6/8 & detection \\\\\n1.00 & 6/8 & 6/8 & detection \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Consensus threshold operating characteristics ($K \\in \\{2,3,4,5\\}$). FPR: null-control rate (9 seeds, 54 pairs); TPR: detection rate at selected $\\alpha$ levels (Pythia-410M, $T{=}10$, fp32). $K{=}3$ balances FPR control (11.1\\%) against detection power (75\\% at $\\alpha{=}1.0$); $K{=}4$ eliminates false positives but reduces TPR to ${\\leq}25\\%$.}\n\\label{tab:mmct_k_operating}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\n& $K{\\geq}2$ & $K{\\geq}3$ & $K{\\geq}4$ & $K{=}5$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nFPR ($\\alpha{=}0.0$) & 38.9\\% & 11.1\\% & 0\\% & 0\\% \\\\\nTPR ($\\alpha{=}0.50$) & 85.4\\% & 66.7\\% & 25.0\\% & 4.2\\% \\\\\nTPR ($\\alpha{=}0.75$) & 72.9\\% & 37.5\\% & 0\\% & 0\\% \\\\\nTPR ($\\alpha{=}1.0$) & 75.0\\% & 75.0\\% & 25.0\\% & 0\\% \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n",

    "newString": "\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_spec_curve.pdf}\n\\caption{Specification curve (1{,}160 configurations). The blind$\\to$onset jump at $\\alpha_c{\\approx}0.50$ is robust across methods, pairs, and seeds.}\n\\label{fig:spec_curve}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Detection outcomes across contamination levels (Pythia-410M, seed~42, $T{=}10$, fp32). PermG: forward FDR-significant pairs; $K{\\geq}3$: consensus pairs (of 8 canary$\\to$downstream pairs).}\n\\label{tab:summary_detection}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}\n\\toprule\n$\\alpha$ & PermG & $K{\\geq}3$ & Regime \\\\\n\\midrule\n0.00 & 0/8 & 0/8 & null \\\\\n0.25 & 0/8 & 0/8 & blind \\\\\n0.45 & 0/8 & 0/8 & blind \\\\\n0.50 & 6/8 & 6/8 & onset \\\\\n0.60 & 0/8 & 0/8 & anomaly \\\\\n0.75 & 6/8 & 6/8 & detection \\\\\n1.00 & 6/8 & 6/8 & detection \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\subsection{Dose-Response and Detection Regimes}",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:experiments}\n\n\\subsection{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{sec:setup}\n\nWe study iterative training collapse using Pythia-410M~\\citep{biderman2023pythia} as the primary architecture.\nTraining data is drawn from WikiText-103~\\citep{merity2017pointer} following the replace paradigm of~\\citet{shumailov2024curse}: each generation fine-tunes on a mixture of $\\alpha$ synthetic and $(1{-}\\alpha)$ real data, generates 5{,}000 replacement samples, and fully replaces the synthetic pool (hyperparameters in Appendix~\\ref{app:training_details}).\nWe run $T{=}10$ generations across 13 contamination levels ($\\alpha \\in \\{0.00, \\ldots, 1.00\\}$) with multi-seed replication, yielding ${\\sim}1{,}500$ statistical tests after BH-FDR correction~\\citep{benjamini1995controlling}.\nWe track two \\emph{canary metrics} (token entropy and ECE) and three \\emph{downstream metrics}: distinct-1, distinct-2~\\citep{li2016diversity}, and perplexity (distinct-3: Appendix~\\ref{app:distinct3}).\nPermG uses 5{,}000 permutations on first-differenced series~\\citep{shojaie2022granger} with maximum lag~5.\n\n\n\\subsection{Sharp Detection Onset}\n\\label{sec:regime}\n\nDiversity collapse occurs at every contamination level: distinct-1 declines by $14.1\\%$ even at $\\alpha{=}0.0$ (fine-tuning drift) and by $40.3{\\pm}0.3\\%$ at $\\alpha{=}0.25$.\nDespite this ubiquitous degradation, Granger detectability is sharply regime-dependent.\nPermG finds 0/8 canary$\\to$downstream pairs FDR-significant from $\\alpha{=}0.00$ through $\\alpha{=}0.45$, then jumps to 6/8 (Clopper--Pearson 95\\% CI $[0.25, 0.92]$) at $\\alpha{=}0.50$, uniformly across all 3~fp32 seeds.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/metric_trajectory.pdf}\n\\caption{Metric trajectories at $\\alpha{=}1.0$ (Pythia-410M, 4~seeds). Token entropy drops at generation~1; distinct-1 declines from generations~2--3, with a consistent one-generation canary lead (shaded: inter-seed range).}\n\\label{fig:metric_trajectory}\n\\end{figure}\n\nThis transition is striking because cumulative synthetic fraction barely changes across the boundary ($C{=}99.75\\%$ at $\\alpha{=}0.45$ vs.\\ $99.90\\%$ at $\\alpha{=}0.50$), yet detection jumps from null to reliable.\nSub-threshold $p$-values at $\\alpha{=}0.45$ ($p_{\\mathrm{fdr}} = 0.059$--$0.091$) confirm proximity to the boundary.\nSigmoid regression yields $\\alpha_c = 0.483 \\pm 0.006$ ($R^2 = 0.969$; Figure~\\ref{fig:dose_response}); cross-seed consistency (3/3 seeds: $0/8{\\to}6/8$) and specification-invariance across 1{,}160 configurations (Appendix~\\ref{app:spec_curve}) argue for a genuine transition (Fisher exact: $p = 3.45 \\times 10^{-11}$).\n\n\\paragraph{Canary scoping.}\nFirst-differencing scopes the canary claim precisely.\nCanary$\\to$diversity pairs retain unanimous FDR significance after first-differencing ($24/24 \\to 24/24$ across 4~seeds at $\\alpha{=}1.0$), while canary$\\to$perplexity collapses entirely ($F \\approx 0$, $p = 0.99$)~\\citep{granger1974spurious}.\nAt $\\alpha{=}1.0$, all 24 canary$\\to$diversity pairs reach FDR significance in the forward direction with 0/24 reverse; entropy onset at generation~1 precedes distinct-$n$ at generation~2 in 16/18 WikiText conditions (Appendix~\\ref{app:moment_cascade}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[htb]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_cascade_onset.pdf}\n\\caption{Cascade onset timeline (Pythia-410M, WikiText, fp32). Entropy and ECE reach onset at generation~1; distinct-$n$ follows at generation~2. The ordering $t_H \\leq t_D$ holds across all conditions.}\n\\label{fig:cascade_onset}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{Blind zone.}\nAt $\\alpha{=}0.10$, distinct-1 has already dropped by $39.6\\%$, yet token entropy is indistinguishable from the null.\nBelow $\\alpha_c$ at $T{=}10$, diversity collapse proceeds without any detectable Granger-precedence signal.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_spec_curve.pdf}\n\\caption{Specification curve (1{,}160 configurations). The blind$\\to$onset jump at $\\alpha_c{\\approx}0.50$ is robust across methods, pairs, and seeds.}\n\\label{fig:spec_curve}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\n\\subsection{Dose-Response and Detection Regimes}\n\\label{sec:dose_response}\n\nThe PermG dose-response profile (Figure~\\ref{fig:dose_response}; Appendix~\\ref{app:dose_response}) reveals three confirmed regimes---\\emph{blind} ($\\alpha \\leq 0.45$, 0/8), \\emph{onset} ($\\alpha \\approx 0.50$, 6/8), and \\emph{detection} ($\\alpha{\\geq}0.75$, 6/8)---plus a non-monotonic anomaly at $\\alpha \\approx 0.60$ (0/8).\nAt this level, all direction-checked methods fail (PermG 0/8, threshold onset 0/8, Toda--Yamamoto 1/8), while bidirectional DiffXCorr maintains detection (6/8), suggesting compressed temporal lag disrupts directional separation.\nThe anomaly dissolves by $T{=}25$ ($5/8$), consistent with $\\sqrt{T}$ power scaling (Appendix~\\ref{app:t20_comparison}).\n\n\\FloatBarrier\n\n\\subsection{Detection Boundary Left-Shift}\n\\label{sec:t20}\n\nLonger observation windows push the detection boundary leftward: $\\alpha_c(5){\\geq}1.0$, $\\alpha_c(10){=}0.487$, consistent with power-law scaling (\\S\\ref{sec:theory}).\nThe $\\alpha{=}0.45$ column validates this: undetectable at $T{\\leq}10$ (0/8), it crosses into reliable detection at $T{=}15$ (6/8, dual-seed) and remains there at $T{=}20$ and $T{=}25$.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_dose_response.pdf}\n\\caption{Dose-response: PermG detection rate with sigmoid fit ($\\alpha_c{=}0.483$; shaded 95\\% CI). Regime bands mark blind, onset, sync-dip, and detection zones.}\n\\label{fig:dose_response}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Architecture Validation}\n\\label{sec:generalization}\n\nWe evaluate PermG at $\\alpha{=}1.0$ across 15 model-seed combinations spanning five families (345M--6.9B) and two domains (Table~\\ref{tab:generalization}; Appendix~\\ref{app:cross_arch_table}).\nForward detection reaches ${\\geq}6/8$ in 11/15 conditions (Figure~\\ref{fig:cross_arch_heatmap}).\nThe four non-confirming conditions each isolate a distinct failure mode: precision sensitivity (GPT-2 bf16: 0/8 vs.\\ fp32: 6/8), bidirectional confounding (GPT-2 s43), seed dependence (Pythia-1.4B: s42 2/8, s43 6/8), and entropy decoupling (SmolLM3-3B: 3/8).\nStandalone PermG claims require architecture-specific null calibration (GPT-2 null FPR: 50\\%; Appendix~\\ref{app:fpr_independence}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[htb]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_cross_arch_heatmap.pdf}\n\\caption{Cross-architecture validation ($\\alpha{=}1.0$): PermG forward detection (${\\geq}6/8$) in 11/15 model-seed conditions spanning five families (345M--6.9B); faded bars: non-confirming conditions.}\n\\label{fig:cross_arch_heatmap}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\n\\subsection{Canary-Based Intervention (Proof of Concept)}\n\\label{sec:intervention}\n\nAs a proof-of-concept under favorable conditions ($\\alpha{=}1.0$), we implement predictive cessation: training switches to pure real data when token entropy drops ${>}15\\%$ from generation~0 (robust band $[10\\%, 22\\%]$; Appendix~\\ref{app:intervention_sensitivity}).\nThe predictive trigger fires at generation~1 (one generation before a reactive distinct-1 baseline), reducing peak perplexity by 64\\% ($56.99$ vs.\\ $157.09$; Figure~\\ref{fig:intervention}) in both Pythia-410M and 1.4B.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_intervention.pdf}\n\\caption{Canary-triggered intervention (Pythia-410M, $\\alpha{=}1.0$). Predictive cessation on entropy (generation~1) reduces peak perplexity by 64\\% versus reactive triggering on distinct-1 (generation~2).}\n\\label{fig:intervention}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Detection outcomes across contamination levels (Pythia-410M, seed~42, $T{=}10$, fp32). PermG: forward FDR-significant pairs; $K{\\geq}3$: consensus pairs (of 8 canary$\\to$downstream pairs).}\n\\label{tab:summary_detection}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}\n\\toprule\n$\\alpha$ & PermG & $K{\\geq}3$ & Regime \\\\\n\\midrule\n0.00 & 0/8 & 0/8 & null \\\\\n0.25 & 0/8 & 0/8 & blind \\\\\n0.45 & 0/8 & 0/8 & blind \\\\\n0.50 & 6/8 & 6/8 & onset \\\\\n0.60 & 0/8 & 0/8 & anomaly \\\\\n0.75 & 6/8 & 6/8 & detection \\\\\n1.00 & 6/8 & 6/8 & detection \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Consensus threshold operating characteristics ($K \\in \\{2,3,4,5\\}$). FPR: null-control rate (9 seeds, 54 pairs); TPR: detection rate at selected $\\alpha$ levels (Pythia-410M, $T{=}10$, fp32). $K{=}3$ balances FPR control (11.1\\%) against detection power (75\\% at $\\alpha{=}1.0$); $K{=}4$ eliminates false positives but reduces TPR to ${\\leq}25\\%$.}\n\\label{tab:mmct_k_operating}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\n& $K{\\geq}2$ & $K{\\geq}3$ & $K{\\geq}4$ & $K{=}5$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nFPR ($\\alpha{=}0.0$) & 38.9\\% & 11.1\\% & 0\\% & 0\\% \\\\\nTPR ($\\alpha{=}0.50$) & 85.4\\% & 66.7\\% & 25.0\\% & 4.2\\% \\\\\nTPR ($\\alpha{=}0.75$) & 72.9\\% & 37.5\\% & 0\\% & 0\\% \\\\\nTPR ($\\alpha{=}1.0$) & 75.0\\% & 75.0\\% & 25.0\\% & 0\\% \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n",

PermG: forward FDR-significant pairs; $K{\\geq}3$: consensus pairs (of 8 canary$\\to$downstream pairs).}\n\\label{tab:summary_detection}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}\n\\toprule\n$\\alpha$ & PermG & $K{\\geq}3$ & Regime \\\\\n\\midrule\n0.00 & 0/8 & 0/8 & null \\\\\n0.25 & 0/8 & 0/8 & blind \\\\\n0.45 & 0/8 & 0/8 & blind \\\\\n0.50 & 6/8 & 6/8 & onset \\\\\n0.60 & 0/8 & 0/8 & anomaly \\\\\n0.75 & 6/8 & 6/8 & detection \\\\\n1.00 & 6/8 & 6/8 & detection \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:experiments}\n\n\\subsection{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{sec:setup}\n\nWe study iterative training collapse using Pythia-410M~\\citep{biderman2023pythia} as the primary architecture.\nTraining data is drawn from WikiText-103~\\citep{merity2017pointer} following the replace paradigm of~\\citet{shumailov2024curse}: each generation fine-tunes on a mixture of $\\alpha$ synthetic and $(1{-}\\alpha)$ real data, generates 5{,}000 replacement samples, and fully replaces the synthetic pool (hyperparameters in Appendix~\\ref{app:training_details}).\nWe run $T{=}10$ generations across 13 contamination levels ($\\alpha \\in \\{0.00, \\ldots, 1.00\\}$) with multi-seed replication, yielding ${\\sim}1{,}500$ statistical tests after BH-FDR correction~\\citep{benjamini1995controlling}.\nWe track two \\emph{canary metrics} (token entropy and ECE) and three \\emph{downstream metrics}: distinct-1, distinct-2~\\citep{li2016diversity}, and perplexity (distinct-3: Appendix~\\ref{app:distinct3}).\nPermG uses 5{,}000 permutations on first-differenced series~\\citep{shojaie2022granger} with maximum lag~5.\n\n\n\\subsection{Sharp Detection Onset}\n\\label{sec:regime}\n\nDiversity collapse occurs at every contamination level: distinct-1 declines by $14.1\\%$ even at $\\alpha{=}0.0$ (fine-tuning drift) and by $40.3{\\pm}0.3\\%$ at $\\alpha{=}0.25$.\nDespite this ubiquitous degradation, Granger detectability is sharply regime-dependent.\nPermG finds 0/8 canary$\\to$downstream pairs FDR-significant from $\\alpha{=}0.00$ through $\\alpha{=}0.45$, then jumps to 6/8 (Clopper--Pearson 95\\% CI $[0.25, 0.92]$) at $\\alpha{=}0.50$, uniformly across all 3~fp32 seeds.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/metric_trajectory.pdf}\n\\caption{Metric trajectories at $\\alpha{=}1.0$ (Pythia-410M, 4~seeds). Token entropy drops at generation~1; distinct-1 declines from generations~2--3, with a consistent one-generation canary lead (shaded: inter-seed range).}\n\\label{fig:metric_trajectory}\n\\end{figure}\n\nThis transition is striking because cumulative synthetic fraction barely changes across the boundary ($C{=}99.75\\%$ at $\\alpha{=}0.45$ vs.\\ $99.90\\%$ at $\\alpha{=}0.50$), yet detection jumps from null to reliable.\nSub-threshold $p$-values at $\\alpha{=}0.45$ ($p_{\\mathrm{fdr}} = 0.059$--$0.091$) confirm proximity to the boundary.\nSigmoid regression yields $\\alpha_c = 0.483 \\pm 0.006$ ($R^2 = 0.969$; Figure~\\ref{fig:dose_response}); cross-seed consistency (3/3 seeds: $0/8{\\to}6/8$) and specification-invariance across 1{,}160 configurations (Appendix~\\ref{app:spec_curve}) argue for a genuine transition (Fisher exact: $p = 3.45 \\times 10^{-11}$).\n\n\\paragraph{Canary scoping.}\nFirst-differencing scopes the canary claim precisely.\nCanary$\\to$diversity pairs retain unanimous FDR significance after first-differencing ($24/24 \\to 24/24$ across 4~seeds at $\\alpha{=}1.0$), while canary$\\to$perplexity collapses entirely ($F \\approx 0$, $p = 0.99$)~\\citep{granger1974spurious}.\nAt $\\alpha{=}1.0$, all 24 canary$\\to$diversity pairs reach FDR significance in the forward direction with 0/24 reverse; entropy onset at generation~1 precedes distinct-$n$ at generation~2 in 16/18 WikiText conditions (Appendix~\\ref{app:moment_cascade}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[htb]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_cascade_onset.pdf}\n\\caption{Cascade onset timeline (Pythia-410M, WikiText, fp32). Entropy and ECE reach onset at generation~1; distinct-$n$ follows at generation~2. The ordering $t_H \\leq t_D$ holds across all conditions.}\n\\label{fig:cascade_onset}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{Blind zone.}\nAt $\\alpha{=}0.10$, distinct-1 has already dropped by $39.6\\%$, yet token entropy is indistinguishable from the null.\nBelow $\\alpha_c$ at $T{=}10$, diversity collapse proceeds without any detectable Granger-precedence signal.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_spec_curve.pdf}\n\\caption{Specification curve (1{,}160 configurations). The blind$\\to$onset jump at $\\alpha_c{\\approx}0.50$ is robust across methods, pairs, and seeds.}\n\\label{fig:spec_curve}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Detection outcomes across contamination levels (Pythia-410M, seed~42, $T{=}10$, fp32). PermG: forward FDR-significant pairs; $K{\\geq}3$: consensus pairs (of 8 canary$\\to$downstream pairs).}\n\\label{tab:summary_detection}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}\n\\toprule\n$\\alpha$ & PermG & $K{\\geq}3$ & Regime \\\\\n\\midrule\n0.00 & 0/8 & 0/8 & null \\\\\n0.25 & 0/8 & 0/8 & blind \\\\\n0.45 & 0/8 & 0/8 & blind \\\\\n0.50 & 6/8 & 6/8 & onset \\\\\n0.60 & 0/8 & 0/8 & anomaly \\\\\n0.75 & 6/8 & 6/8 & detection \\\\\n1.00 & 6/8 & 6/8 & detection \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\subsection{Dose-Response and Detection Regimes}\n\\label{sec:dose_response}\n\nThe PermG dose-response profile (Figure~\\ref{fig:dose_response}; Appendix~\\ref{app:dose_response}) reveals three confirmed regimes---\\emph{blind} ($\\alpha \\leq 0.45$, 0/8), \\emph{onset} ($\\alpha \\approx 0.50$, 6/8), and \\emph{detection} ($\\alpha{\\geq}0.75$, 6/8)---plus a non-monotonic anomaly at $\\alpha \\approx 0.60$ (0/8).\nAt this level, all direction-checked methods fail (PermG 0/8, threshold onset 0/8, Toda--Yamamoto 1/8), while bidirectional DiffXCorr maintains detection (6/8), suggesting compressed temporal lag disrupts directional separation.\nThe anomaly dissolves by $T{=}25$ ($5/8$), consistent with $\\sqrt{T}$ power scaling (Appendix~\\ref{app:t20_comparison}).\n\n\\FloatBarrier\n\n\\subsection{Detection Boundary Left-Shift}\n\\label{sec:t20}\n\nLonger observation windows push the detection boundary leftward: $\\alpha_c(5){\\geq}1.0$, $\\alpha_c(10){=}0.487$, consistent with power-law scaling (\\S\\ref{sec:theory}).\nThe $\\alpha{=}0.45$ column validates this: undetectable at $T{\\leq}10$ (0/8), it crosses into reliable detection at $T{=}15$ (6/8, dual-seed) and remains there at $T{=}20$ and $T{=}25$.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_dose_response.pdf}\n\\caption{Dose-response: PermG detection rate with sigmoid fit ($\\alpha_c{=}0.483$; shaded 95\\% CI). Regime bands mark blind, onset, sync-dip, and detection zones.}\n\\label{fig:dose_response}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Architecture Validation}\n\\label{sec:generalization}\n\nWe evaluate PermG at $\\alpha{=}1.0$ across 15 model-seed combinations spanning five families (345M--6.9B) and two domains (Table~\\ref{tab:generalization}; Appendix~\\ref{app:cross_arch_table}).\nForward detection reaches ${\\geq}6/8$ in 11/15 conditions (Figure~\\ref{fig:cross_arch_heatmap}).\nThe four non-confirming conditions each isolate a distinct failure mode: precision sensitivity (GPT-2 bf16: 0/8 vs.\\ fp32: 6/8), bidirectional confounding (GPT-2 s43), seed dependence (Pythia-1.4B: s42 2/8, s43 6/8), and entropy decoupling (SmolLM3-3B: 3/8).\nStandalone PermG claims require architecture-specific null calibration (GPT-2 null FPR: 50\\%; Appendix~\\ref{app:fpr_independence}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[htb]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_cross_arch_heatmap.pdf}\n\\caption{Cross-architecture validation ($\\alpha{=}1.0$): PermG forward detection (${\\geq}6/8$) in 11/15 model-seed conditions spanning five families (345M--6.9B); faded bars: non-confirming conditions.}\n\\label{fig:cross_arch_heatmap}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\n\\subsection{Canary-Based Intervention (Proof of Concept)}\n\\label{sec:intervention}\n\nAs a proof-of-concept under favorable conditions ($\\alpha{=}1.0$), we implement predictive cessation: training switches to pure real data when token entropy drops ${>}15\\%$ from generation~0 (robust band $[10\\%, 22\\%]$; Appendix~\\ref{app:intervention_sensitivity}).\nThe predictive trigger fires at generation~1 (one generation before a reactive distinct-1 baseline), reducing peak perplexity by 64\\% ($56.99$ vs.\\ $157.09$; Figure~\\ref{fig:intervention}) in both Pythia-410M and 1.4B.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_intervention.pdf}\n\\caption{Canary-triggered intervention (Pythia-410M, $\\alpha{=}1.0$). Predictive cessation on entropy (generation~1) reduces peak perplexity by 64\\% versus reactive triggering on distinct-1 (generation~2).}\n\\label{fig:intervention}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Detection outcomes across contamination levels (Pythia-410M, seed~42, $T{=}10$, fp32). PermG: forward FDR-significant pairs; $K{\\geq}3$: consensus pairs (of 8 canary$\\to$downstream pairs).}\n\\label{tab:summary_detection}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}\n\\toprule\n$\\alpha$ & PermG & $K{\\geq}3$ & Regime \\\\\n\\midrule\n0.00 & 0/8 & 0/8 & null \\\\\n0.25 & 0/8 & 0/8 & blind \\\\\n0.45 & 0/8 & 0/8 & blind \\\\\n0.50 & 6/8 & 6/8 & onset \\\\\n0.60 & 0/8 & 0/8 & anomaly \\\\\n0.75 & 6/8 & 6/8 & detection \\\\\n1.00 & 6/8 & 6/8 & detection \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Consensus threshold operating characteristics ($K \\in \\{2,3,4,5\\}$). FPR: null-control rate (9 seeds, 54 pairs); TPR: detection rate at selected $\\alpha$ levels (Pythia-410M, $T{=}10$, fp32). $K{=}3$ balances FPR control (11.1\\%) against detection power (75\\% at $\\alpha{=}1.0$); $K{=}4$ eliminates false positives but reduces TPR to ${\\leq}25\\%$.}\n\\label{tab:mmct_k_operating}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\n& $K{\\geq}2$ & $K{\\geq}3$ & $K{\\geq}4$ & $K{=}5$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nFPR ($\\alpha{=}0.0$) & 38.9\\% & 11.1\\% & 0\\% & 0\\% \\\\\nTPR ($\\alpha{=}0.50$) & 85.4\\% & 66.7\\% & 25.0\\% & 4.2\\% \\\\\nTPR ($\\alpha{=}0.75$) & 72.9\\% & 37.5\\% & 0\\% & 0\\% \\\\\nTPR ($\\alpha{=}1.0$) & 75.0\\% & 75.0\\% & 25.0\\% & 0\\% \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n",

### Assistant

_source line 5119 · 2026-05-24T21:02:23.838Z_

Moving Table 1 earlier helped. Final distribution:

| Page | Figures | Tables | Content |
|------|---------|--------|---------|
| P1 | 1 (phase diagram) | 0 | Intro |
| P2 | 0 | 0 | Intro + Related Work |
| P3 | 1 (method comparison) | 0 | Method §3.1-3.3 |
| P4 | 2 (detection surface, metric trajectory) | 0 | Method §3.4 + Experiments §4.1-4.2 |
| P5 | 2 (cascade onset, spec curve) | 1 (Table 1) | §4.2 cont + §4.3-4.4 |
| P6 | 3 (dose-response, cross-arch, intervention) | 0 | §4.4-4.6 + Discussion |
| P7 | 0 | 1 (Table 2) | Discussion cont + Conclusion + Limitations + Refs |

P6 still has 3 figures, but it's the densest experimental section (dose-response, cross-arch, intervention) so 3 figures is natural there. P2 is text-only but it contains the contributions list + Related Work which is inherently text-heavy. This is much better than the original 7-figure P5-P6 cluster.

[Director 指令] 用户反馈：主文 7 页太少，差 1 页才到 8 页限制。需要充实内容到接近 8 页。

**目标**：主文从 7 页扩充到 7.8-8.0 页（Conclusion/Limitations 在第 8 页底部或溢出到第 9 页顶部）。

**扩充方案（按价值排序）**：

**1. MMCT 算法伪代码提升到 Method（~0.3 页）**
- 把附录中的 MMCT Algorithm 伪代码（Algorithm 1）移到 method.tex §3.3 附近
- 这是论文的核心方法，放正文比放附录更合理
- 附录改为反向引用

**2. Discussion 扩展（~0.3 页）**
- 从附录的 partial correlation 分析中提取关键结论：diversity canary 有效的机制解释（entropy 领先 diversity，但 perplexity 有 spurious regression 风险）
- 从 Shapley decomposition 提取：哪些方法贡献最大、方法组合的互补性
- 这些是审稿人会关心的"为什么"问题，放 Discussion 自然

**3. Cross-Architecture 结果展开（~0.2 页）**
- 当前 §4.5 只有 1-2 句话提及 1.4B scale 和跨家族结果
- 展开讨论：scale dimension（410M vs 1.4B 的检测边界差异/一致性）、不同 model family 的 failure modes（4 个 non-confirming conditions 各自的原因）
- 可以从附录的 1.4B phase 表和 cross-arch 表中提取关键数据点

**4. Related Work 适当扩充（~0.2 页）**
- 3 个 subsection 各加 1-2 句，补充重要的相关工作引用
- 特别是 §2.2（Detection）和 §2.3（Statistical Methods）可以多覆盖几篇

**硬约束**：
- 主文（Abstract→Conclusion）≤ 8 页
- Limitations 不计入
- 不要为凑字数而水内容——每句话都要有信息量
- 编译后确认页数，目标 7.8-8.0 页

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_013h5jrWJLMgGkGpthHNL1kQ`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related_work}
3	
4	\subsection{Model Collapse: Theory and Empirical Evidence}
5	\label{sec:rw_collapse}
6	
7	Iterative training collapse was formalized by \citet{shumailov2024curse} and \citet{alemohammad2024mad}; \citet{schaeffer2025position} systematize eight distinct notions of collapse---our operationalization aligns with their \emph{distributional shift} family (definitions~D4--D6).
8	Theoretical analyses sharpen these results: \citet{dohmatob2024tale} recast collapse as changed scaling-law exponents, \citet{dohmatob2025strong} proved collapse at vanishingly small synthetic fractions, and \citet{truong2026phase} prove entropy collapse is a first-order transition, structurally ruling out critical-slowing-down early-warning signals~\citep{scheffer2009early,scheffer2012anticipating}.
9	Critical mixture ratios separating qualitative outcomes have independent precedent: \citet{kazdan2025collapse} map collapse conditions, \citet{gu2024cmr} establish scaling laws for critical ratios, and \citet{gu2025datamixing} show data mixing induces phase transitions in knowledge acquisition.
10	
11	\subsection{Detection of Synthetic Data Contamination}
12	\label{sec:rw_detection}
13	
14	Monitoring metrics proposed for collapse include expected calibration error (ECE)~\citep{du2025scales}, surplexity~\citep{gambetta2024surplexity}, and machine-generated text detection~\citep{drayson2025detection}; \citet{shi2025closer} independently validate entropy as a collapse diagnostic in diffusion models.
15	However, these works evaluate single metrics in isolation without cross-validating against alternative statistical tests.
16	We differ by applying five detection methods to identical data, mapping detectability across contamination regimes, and treating method sensitivity as a primary finding rather than a robustness check (detailed comparison: Appendix~\ref{app:method_comparison}).
17	
18	\subsection{Statistical Methods for Distribution Shift}
19	\label{sec:rw_methods}
20	
21	For temporal precedence testing, Granger causality~\citep{granger1969investigating} is the standard framework, with neural extensions~\citep{tank2021neural,runge2019detecting}; however, non-stationary series inflate rejection rates~\citep{granger1974spurious}, and short series ($T{\leq}25$) preclude reliable nonlinear estimation~\citep{boettiger2012limits}.
22	Granger precedence establishes temporal predictive improvement, not true causation: bivariate tests cannot exclude confounders~\citep{shojaie2022granger}.
23	Our permutation-based approach circumvents the critical-slowing-down limitation by detecting temporal predictive improvement rather than variance or autocorrelation signatures.
24	The multiverse analysis perspective~\citep{steegen2016increasing,coretta2023multidimensional,kapoor2024reforms} motivates our multi-method comparison: five methods on identical data produce conclusions ranging from universal significance to complete null, paralleling analyst-dependent divergence documented in many-analysts studies~\citep{coretta2023multidimensional}.
25	
```

## MMCT Algorithm Pseudocode

**File:** `/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_4/projects/distrib_canary_collapse/docs/paper/appendix.tex`

**Section Location:** Lines 170-192

**Section Label:** `\label{app:mmct}` (line 171)

### Complete Content (lines 170-192):

```latex
\section{MMCT Algorithm}
\label{app:mmct}

Algorithm~\ref{alg:mmct} describes the Multi-Method Consensus Test (MMCT) introduced in \S\ref{sec:mmct}.

\begin{algorithm}[h]
\caption{Multi-Method Consensus Test (MMCT)}
\label{alg:mmct}
\begin{algorithmic}[1]
\Require Time series pair $(X, Y)$; methods $\{m_1, \ldots, m_K\}$ ($K{=}5$); null-control data $\mathcal{N}$ (from $\alpha{=}0.0$); tolerance $\epsilon{=}0.01$
\Ensure $p$-value, consensus level $\hat{K}$
\For{each method $m_k$}
    \State Compute empirical FPR: $\widehat{\mathrm{FPR}}_k \gets$ fraction of null-control pairs where $m_k$ rejects $H_0$
    \State Compute weight: $w_k \gets 1 / \max(\widehat{\mathrm{FPR}}_k,\; \epsilon)$
    \State Apply $m_k$ to $(X, Y)$: $d_k \gets \mathbf{1}[m_k \text{ rejects } H_0]$
\EndFor
\State Weighted consensus: $W(X {\to} Y) \gets \sum_{k} w_k \, d_k \;/\; \sum_{k} w_k$
\State Build empirical null distribution of $W$ from all null-control pairs in $\mathcal{N}$
\State Compute $p$-value from empirical null: $p \gets \Pr_{\mathcal{N}}(W \geq W_{\mathrm{obs}})$
\State Consensus level: $\hat{K} \gets \sum_{k} d_k$
\State \Return $p$, $\hat{K}$
\end{algorithmic}
\end{algorithm}
```

### Design Rationale Section (lines 194-197):

Following the algorithm is an explanatory paragraph that describes the design rationale:

```latex
\paragraph{Design rationale.}
FPR-inverse weighting penalizes methods with high false positive rates on null-control data: naive cross-correlation (FPR${=}94.4\%$) receives weight $w{=}1.06$, while PermG (FPR${=}7.4\%$, the lowest among all five methods) receives $w{=}13.5$.
The empirical null distribution avoids parametric assumptions that are unreliable for short ($T{=}5$--$20$), non-stationary series typical of iterative training.
MMCT is a consensus testing protocol, not a novel statistical method; its value lies in systematic method comparison and the identification of method-dependent conclusions.
```
````
